![](https://i.ormos.uk/6e/b1/6eb189f1638e0712367dade8e1d0ea1db5ae36ab0faf46becbb3a0655db15e7f.jpg)

This weekend millions of players across the globe will be tearing open brand new booster packs from the new Ravnica: City of Guilds expansion. In Russia things are a little different. Over here we have the 9th Edition release. This is not just any old release. This tournament marks the first expansion of Magic to be released using the Cyrillic alphabet. To commemorate the occasion sixteen of the World's best players have been invited over to take on the best that Russia, Ukraine and Belarus can offer in an exhibition tournament. For the full player list and profiles see here.

In the end the Local Heroes were not enough to stand up to the might of the Best of the Rest. The Best ran out 129-95 winners. But there was still the matter of the rotisserie draft for the top four players of each team.

Julien Nuijten was the top ranked player from Saturday and had the honor of taking the first pick in the Rotisserie draft. This draft featured every card in 9th Edition, in foil. Unsurprisingly, the World Champion took [Loxodon Warhammer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Warhammer). It was not enough to save him from Antoine Ruel's clever, almost creature-less, blue-red deck in the semi-finals.

On the other side of the bracket Matvey Linov, perhaps better known as DuSheLov from Magic Online, was dismantling his opponents with an almost mono-blue deck. In the end it was the Russian player who caused the upset in the finals. His [Mahamoti Djinn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mahamoti+Djinn) proved to be too tough to kill for the experienced French pro. The Local Heroes team might have been defeated by the Best of the Rest, but they were able to salvage some pride as Matvey Linov came out on top of the Rotisserie draft.

Overall the event was a massive success with many local players showing up to play in the 9th Edition release or to test their skills against the pro's on the gunslinging tables.

## BLOG

## Sunday, September 25: 2:23 pm - Gunslingers of Moscow

![](https://i.ormos.uk/19/09/19091991ad644e4ca4ab1e1d396b8c2f3c775f3637ce3d3974c43f16c98d6ca9.jpg)

The Moscow Invitational is a pretty sweet deal for the Pro's invited. They get their expenses paid, a bit of spending money and they get to see the sights of Moscow. It isn't totally free though as one of the requirements is that the players who don't make the draft have to turn up today and gunsling. This is a bit meaner than the usual form of the gunslinging as all the pro's get to work with is five measly boosters of Ninth Edition. Unfortunately the locals aren't playing to the script and coming with all sorts of decks.

I asked VP of TCG's Joe Hauck how it was going. He muttered something about a Psychatog deck. Oysp said he'd almost managed to pull it out against an Extended Survival of the Fittest deck, but his [Naturalize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Naturalize) ran into Force of Will. Masashi Oiso might be the best player in the world at the moment but I don't think he's beating Survival with a 9th Edition sealed deck either. Olivier found a convenient excuse (the third place matchup) just as a Balancing Act deck was setting up to demolish the world.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/79/c1/79c1cc072986714052bae4c0307e2653477c87cf83fdff21175b54dabb76e4bb.jpg)

You'd think they'd at least be more sporting. I dunno, maybe let the Pro's start with three land in play, or 40 life, or ten cards.

I suppose it's all therapeutic. Come to Moscow. Crush a Pro.

\*update\*

Anton Jonsson beat an Extended deck. But as it was a Balancing Tings deck I suppose that doesn't count.

## Sunday, September 25: 6:21 pm - Top 8 Draft Report

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/top8.jpg)*The teams prepare to draft*

And this is the moment you've been waiting for. Given the choice of every card in 9th Edition, which card should you take first? Well there weren't too many surprises. Julien Nuijten, as the top ranked player in the swiss, got to choose first and rather predictably chose [Loxodon Warhammer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Warhammer). As an indication of how powerful equipment is in the post-*Mirrodin* environment Gerasimenko immediately took [Vulshok Morningstar](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vulshok+Morningstar). Antoine Ruel also plumped for an artifact, [Icy Manipulator](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Icy+Manipulator), before some people's top pick, [Wrath of God](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wrath+of+God), went fourth. Cornelissen opted for [Royal Assassin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Royal+Assassin); Alieksieiev, [Worship](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Worship); Olivier Ruel, [Plague Wind](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plague+Wind) and then Linov wheeled [Confiscate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Confiscate) and [Mahamoti Djinn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mahamoti+Djinn).

Before the draft Olivier Ruel had said he'd wanted to build around [Hell's Caretaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Caretaker)/[Nekrataal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nekrataal) Combo and also pick up Pague Wind and [Grave Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Pact). I didn't think he'd get all four but he did. He also got [Gravedigger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravedigger), Rats, [Highway Robber](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Highway+Robber) … you get the idea where this going. [Hell's Caretaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Caretaker) would be a kill on sight card against his deck.

His brother Antoine, and quarter-final opponent grabbed a [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon) second pick, but then steered away from creatures. He built a blue-red deck with burn, counters and card drawing and grabbed support from artifacts. With [Traumatize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Traumatize) and [Aladdin's Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aladdin%27s+Ring) it was interesting looking deck.

Julien Nuijten looked to capitalize on the Warhammer with a very fast red-white deck with weenies and [Glorious Anthem](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glorious+Anthem). He also got [Flame Wave](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame+Wave) and [Shard Phoenix](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shard+Phoenix), but left [Seething Song](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seething+Song) too late and saw someone else snatch it. I only mention [Seething Song](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seething+Song) because Nuijten had drafted three of then in a deck that featured [Flame Wave](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame+Wave) and [Form of the Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Form+of+the+Dragon) earlier this week, which only goes to show that the good players can always find a use for cards that don't immediately seem good. Fellow Dutchmen Cornelissen assembled a solid black deck.

Denis Tagunov seemed to have a solid plan when he started with [Wrath of God](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wrath+of+God) and [Millstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Millstone). I thought he was going for a "stall the game out, deck you" kind of deck but then seemed to lose focus and get pulled into both green and blue.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/1pick.jpg)*Nuijten secures the first pick of the draft*

Green seemed to be universally shunned until several players moved into it late and even then I was surprised at the order of the picks. I think the general depth of the green common creatures loses its appeal when you have access to every rare and uncommon first. [Silklash Spider](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silklash+Spider) was the highest green rare pick with [Ancient Silverback](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Silverback) being snapped up later.

Linov seemed to have almost free access to blue and wheeled [Slay](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slay) and [Execute](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Execute) when they were available later.

Some cards went surprisingly late. Poor old [Serra Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Angel). She might be one of the poster cards but the lady took a long time to find a date in this draft.

  

## Sunday, September 25: 6:43 pm - Quarterfinals: Antoine Ruel vs. Olivier Ruel

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/qf1_1.jpg)*These two brothers came all the way to Moscow to play each other*

The two brothers Ruel get to battle it out in the quarter-finals. The draft was interesting as Olivier got to form the virtually mono-black [Hell's Caretaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Caretaker), [Nekrataal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nekrataal), [Grave Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Pact) deck. Antoine responded by not drafting any creatures other than a [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon) and [Anarchist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anarchist), relying instead on burn or [Traumatize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Traumatize) backed up with counters to win his games.

Antoine won the coin flip and opted to play first. His deck doesn't really do a great deal early. Olivier started with a [Festering Goblin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Festering+Goblin) and then dropped a dangerous card in this matchup, [Nantuko Husk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nantuko+Husk). Antoine tried to burn it with first Guerilla Tactics and then [Shock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shock). The Husk munched down on a [Festering Goblin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Festering+Goblin) and [Looming Shade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Looming+Shade) instead as Olivier kept a Will-O-the-Wisp in reserve.

A [Quicksand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Quicksand) was thrown at the Husk. It ate some [Highway Robber](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Highway+Robber)s and kept coming as Ruel drew cards off a [Treasure Trove](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treasure+Trove).

[Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm). The Husk ate some [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats) and just kept right on coming.

Antoine drew for some answers with [Counsel of the Soratami](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counsel+of+the+Soratami). He was down to four life.

Ruel went for a [Serpent Warrior](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serpent+Warrior) and Antoine had to [Rewind](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rewind) it. Olivier swung with the Husk but didn't try to end the game immediately as his only remaining creature was the Wisp and he didn't want to risk a trick in response.

Antoine got him to sacrifice the Wisp to keep the Husk alive after it took a [Hammer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hammer) on the chin. A [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon) wasn't good enough as Olivier was holding onto a [Consume Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consume+Spirit).

Olivier 1-0 Antoine

Olivier led off with [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats) and then [Drudge Skeletons](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Drudge+Skeletons) as Antoine dropped a [Disrupting Scepter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Scepter) and then ramped up his mana with [Ur-Golem's Eye](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ur-Golem%27s+Eye). Antoine Rewound a [Horror of Horrors](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Horror+of+Horrors) and got to work on Olivier's hand with the Scepter as Olivier only had the two one power beaters.

A [Nekrataal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nekrataal) came out as an expensive 2/1 first striker and took a [Blaze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blaze) on the chin.

The pace of the match was fairly breath taking. Either that or I'm way too slow a typist.

The game state was Olivier with just some [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats) after [Quicksand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Quicksand) sucked down the Skeletons. Antoine was firmly in command now with [Treasure Trove](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treasure+Trove) and then a [Rod of Ruin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rod+of+Ruin).

[Traumatize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Traumatize) smashed Olivier's library. However, a [Grave Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Pact) meant [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon) wouldn't be administering the coup de grace. With barely any library left Antoine [Annex](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annex)ed Olivier's sole [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains).

A [Serpent Warrior](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serpent+Warrior) was big enough to survive the Rod so Antoine buried it under a [Flowstone Slide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flowstone+Slide).

A [Nightmare](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightmare) showed up late but Antoine was able to bring back [Blaze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blaze) with an [Anarchist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anarchist) and then Olivier ran out of library.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/qf1_2.jpg)*Olivier Ruel*

Olivier 1-1 Antoine

Antoine led off and [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak)ed Olivier's turn two [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats). A [Fellwar Stone](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fellwar+Stone) ramped Antoine up to [Sift](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sift) as Olivier replaced the Rats with [Looming Shade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Looming+Shade).

Olivier fetched back the rats with a Graverobber and shredded Antoine's hand with the Rats and [Mind Rot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mind+Rot). His [Disrupting Scepter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Scepter) took Olivier's Gravepact straight back.

A [Flowstone Slide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flowstone+Slide) buried Olivier's board. And it seemed like Antoine had the game under control. Then his brother ripped [Venerable Monk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Venerable+Monk) and then [Nightmare](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightmare). Antoine dropped a [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon) to intercept only for his brother to perform another stunning rip off the top of his library as [Nekrataal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nekrataal) appeared right on cue.

Antoine pushed back his chair in shock before picking up the [Nekrataal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nekrataal) and throwing it to the far side of the room.

I don't know how many times I've written this now in feature match reports.

Olivier is very lucky.

Olivier 2-1 Antoine

My plan was to flit between matches and try and cover as much of the quarter-finals as possible, but with the speed these two play that just isn't possible. Even as I write this they're already five turns into Game 4.

Olivier missed a few land drops but seemed okay with a [Nantuko Husk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nantuko+Husk) until Antoine blazed it out of sight.

An [Annex](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annex) exacerbated the screw and Antoine went for the throat with a [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon).

Olivier found some running lands and was able to [Nekrataal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nekrataal) the dragon. Antoine fetched back [Blaze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blaze) with an [Anarchist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anarchist) and sent a massive hit straight to Olivier's dome.

Olivier was in burn range and used a small [Consume Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consume+Spirit) to pull himself back from the brink. Antoine was now dropping low. He cleared the board with [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm).

A [Hell's Caretaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Caretaker) showed up and promptly switched itself for two precious life from a [Venerable Monk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Venerable+Monk) in the face of Antoine's [Aladdin's Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aladdin%27s+Ring).

It couldn't pull him out of range as a Guerilla Tactics administered the remaining points of damage.

Now it was down to a decider.

Olivier 2-2 Antoine

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/qf1_3.jpg)*Antoine Ruel*

The useful [Nantuko Husk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nantuko+Husk) put in a crucial early appearance from Olivier as Antoine did nothing more than ramp up his mana with an [Ur-Golem's Eye](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ur-Golem%27s+Eye) and [Fellwar Stone](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fellwar+Stone).

The extra mana proved very useful as Antoine as he attempted to bury Olivier's board with [Flowstone Slide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flowstone+Slide). The [Nantuko Husk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nantuko+Husk) toughed it out only for [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats) to hit an unwelcome Guerilla Tactics. Undaunted, Olivier used [Raise Dead](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raise+Dead) to fetch the Husk straight back.

Antoine was not losing control of this one though. An [Icy Manipulator](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Icy+Manipulator) came out to handle the Husk and a [Rod of Ruin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rod+of+Ruin) to shut down the weenie support. With an [Archivist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archivist) out for Antoine, Olivier conceded as he recognized the game had gone away from him (I think Antoine may have showed him a [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon) and [Traumatize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Traumatize) in hand. When I got a chance to look up they were already packing up their cards. Slow motion replay anyone?)

Antoine wins the battle of the brothers Ruel 3-2 and goes onto the semi-finals.

  

## Sunday, September 25: 6:55 pm - Semifinals: Matvey Linov vs. Andrii Alieksieiev

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/sf_linov.jpg)*Matvey Linov*

This match is to determine top dog amongst the Local Heroes in 9th Edition. Linov had close to free reign on the blue cards and assembled a fairly potent blue-black deck. Andrii Alieksieiev started out with [Worship](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Worship) and then moved into green later.

Game 1 was fairly short. Linov came storming out with a [Wind Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wind+Drake) and [Thieving Magpie](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thieving+Magpie) and quickly outdrew his opponent. A [Slay](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slay) on a [Llanowar Behemoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Behemoth) and [Remove Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remove+Soul) on an [Aven Flock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Flock) ended Alieksieiev's chances of catching up.

Linov 1-0 Alieksieiev

Linov again had a first turn [Sleight of Hand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sleight+of+Hand) while his opponent cracked out of the blocks with a Grizzly Bear and [Suntail Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Suntail+Hawk). Linov tried to [Persecute](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Persecute) on white only to miss spectacularly as Alieksieiev was holding a [Blanchwood Armor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blanchwood+Armor) and some land.

Alieksieiev armored up the Hawk and smashed Linov down to 8. Linov was quite happy to two-for-zero him with an [Execute](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Execute).

Undaunted Alieksieiev boosted the bears with [Treetop Bracers](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Bracers) and knocked his opponent down to five. Linov got another big card swing as he [Confiscate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Confiscate)d the Bears.

Alieksieiev followed up with [Llanowar Behemoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Behemoth), [Pegasus Charger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pegasus+Charger) and [Emperor Crocodile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emperor+Crocodile). Still trying to keep on pressure. Unfortunately for him, Linov was getting closer to locking down the game with [Temporal Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Adept) and [Puppeteer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer). A [Mahamoti Djinn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mahamoti+Djinn) shifted the game further in the favor of Linov.

A [Ballista Squad](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ballista+Squad) might have made things interesting, but not against [Puppeteer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer) and [Temporal Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Adept). A few swings with the big blue djinn and Linov was just a game away from the final.

Linov 2-0 Alieksieiev

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/sf_andrii.jpg)*Andrii Alieksieiev*

Alieksieiev led off, but didn't quite find a quick start. His third turn [Paladin en-Vec](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Paladin+en-Vec) was prevented from ever arriving with [Remove Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remove+Soul). Alieksieiev was not about to let it all be one-way traffic. His splashed [Dark Banishing](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Banishing) stopped a [Temporal Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Adept) from causing too much havoc.

A [Persecute](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Persecute) missed again although Linov picked another two-for-nought with [Slay](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slay) on a Braced up [Llanowar Behemoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Behemoth). Only after Linov had [Clone](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Clone)d it first, obviously. When Motty the Djinn entered play Alieksieiev's force of [Viridian Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viridian+Shaman) and [Weathered Wayfarer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Weathered+Wayfarer) seemed a little underpowered by comparison although the life totals were even at 8 a-piece.

Linov wiped him out on the following turn as a [Time Ebb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Ebb) and [Boomerang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boomerang) left Alieksieiev with no creatures left to block the ten points of damage.

Linov could cause an upset against the Best by taking the overall title as his deck looks more than a little useful.

Matvey Linov beats Andrii Alieksieiev 3-0.

  

## Sunday, September 25: 8:40 pm - Final: Matvey Linov vs. Antoine Ruel

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/f_ruel.jpg)*Antoine Ruel*

Welcome to the final of the Moscow Invitational. Matvey Linov and Antoine Ruel have both battled to the top of their respective teams. Yesterday the Best of the Rest mauled the Local Heroes by around a 30 game margin. Matvey Linov can salvage some pride for the Local Heroes by taking the overall title. His deck is pretty good. He had the lion's share of the blue cards and has yet to lose a game in the top 8.

Antoine Ruel drafted intelligently. Seeing that his brother (and quarter-final opponent) was putting together a creature murdering mono-black monstrosity he eschewed creatures completely apart from a [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon) and [Anarchist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anarchist), instead assembling a counter-burn blue-red deck with a hefty card drawing component and some powerful artifacts.

Ruel went first and his deck was predictably quiet in the first couple of turns, making only an [Ur-Golem's Eye](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ur-Golem%27s+Eye).

Linov got cracking on his life total with a [Lumengrid Warden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lumengrid+Warden) and [Phantom Warrior](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phantom+Warrior). Cheekily, Ruel [Annex](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annex)ed Linov's only source of black mana. Linov could fetch it back with his [Boomerang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boomerang) but chose to go for the tempo option instead as his deck is virtually mono-blue in any case.

Linov used the [Boomerang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boomerang) on an [Icy Manipulator](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Icy+Manipulator) that was keeping a [Thieving Magpie](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thieving+Magpie) at bay. That got him a free hit and a card and he used a [Clone](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Clone) to double the number of Magpies. Ruel tried to hold back the beats with a [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon). Linov sent it back to the top of Ruel's library with a [Time Ebb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Ebb) and the tempo loss was too much to come back from for the Frenchman.

I said this deck might cause problems in the final. Ruel had drawn twice as much land as spells though.

Linov 1-0 Ruel

Ruel [Shock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shock)ed an early [Thought Courier](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Courier) and got down his [Treasure Trove](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treasure+Trove) card drawing engine. A [Rewind](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rewind) was waiting for an [Air Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Air+Elemental). Only a temporary setback as Linov [Confiscate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Confiscate)d the [Treasure Trove](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treasure+Trove). Ruel [Annex](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annex)ed an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) straight back. Not really a fair trade.

Linov got to work on Ruel's life total with a [Puppeteer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer) and [Wind Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wind+Drake) while the Frenchman got to work on Linov's hand with a [Disrupting Scepter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Scepter).

A [Wanderguard Sentry](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wanderguard+Sentry) revealed a juicy hand of [Flowstone Slide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flowstone+Slide), [Volcanic Hammer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Hammer) and [Blaze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blaze). Unsurprisingly, Linov had his army buried in Flowstone.

Linov came back at him. First an [Aven Fisher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Fisher) and then, after a [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm), an [Azure Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Drake). A [Mahamoti Djinn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mahamoti+Djinn) joined the party to increase Ruel's woes.

He tried to kill the big flier with a [Blaze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blaze) only for a [Boomerang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boomerang) to mean it was no long there to take the burn. Motty came back and looked like a daunting proposition. A [Hammer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hammer)/Tactics combo accounted for the Drake but the Djinn was just too big. Ruel went looking for answers with an [Archivist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archivist) and found none.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/f_linov.jpg)*Matvey Linov*

Linov 2-0 Ruel.

With one game to go for the title Linov found his deck stalling on him. Ruel tried to exacerbate a man screw with an [Annex](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annex) only for Linov to rescue it with a [Boomerang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boomerang).

Ruel's deck was in full song this game though. [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm) fried a [Thought Courier](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Courier) and then Ruel powered out first [Tidings](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tidings) and then [Sift](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sift) to pick up a full grip of cards. A [Confiscate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Confiscate) was met with [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) and a rain of red removal kept Linov from ever having a creature on the table as Ruel whittled away at his hand with a [Disrupting Scepter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Scepter).

An [Icy Manipulator](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Icy+Manipulator) kept an [Air Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Air+Elemental) occupied while Ruel pinged away with a [Rod of Ruin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rod+of+Ruin). Then his [Shivan Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Dragon) showed up and the Frenchman was back in the match.

Linov 2-1 Ruel.

Linov brought some laugh from the crowd as he led off with a [Fugitive Wizard](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fugitive+Wizard). Ruel bashed one of his lands with a [Stone Rain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stone+Rain), but this time didn't have an [Annex](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annex) to really put the boot in. Linov wasn't really doing a great deal apart from nibbling with his meager 1/1 and so Ruel had time to power out a Scepter and [Ur-Golem's Eye](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ur-Golem%27s+Eye).

Linov found his first substantial threat in the form of an [Air Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Air+Elemental). He [Confiscate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Confiscate)d the Sceptre before he lost the enchantment but I wasn't sure this was the correct target. A [Rod of Ruin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rod+of+Ruin) or [Ur-Golem's Eye](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ur-Golem%27s+Eye) felt like it might have been more damaging.

Guerilla Tactics and [Shock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shock) combined to bring down the [Air Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Air+Elemental). Linov recovered again with a [Sea Monster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sea+Monster) and then a [Clone](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Clone) for a second [Sea Monster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sea+Monster). An [Archivist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archivist) served up a [Flowstone Slide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flowstone+Slide) for Ruel and the board was clear again.

The Scepter was irritating but not really relevant. Ruel sent a massive [Blaze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blaze) at Linov's head rather than losing it. A couple of turns later an [Anarchist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anarchist) allowed him to repeat the trick. Linov's life total wasn't high enough to survive the second blast.

Linov 2-2 Ruel

So now it's down to one game. Linov led off again with the usually awful [Fugitive Wizard](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fugitive+Wizard). Ruel ignored it and fried a [Puppeteer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer) with Guerilla Tactics instead. [Shock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shock) took down a [Wind Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wind+Drake).

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/sideboard/images/rusinv05/f_shake.jpg)*And with a shake the tournament was over!*

A [Wanderguard Sentry](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wanderguard+Sentry) revealed Ruel was short an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island). He found it and stole one of Linov's with [Annex](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annex). The theft was temporary thanks to a [Boomerang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boomerang).  
[Wanderguard Sentry](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wanderguard+Sentry) and [Fugitive Wizard](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fugitive+Wizard) might not be the most exciting of creatures (actually, they're usually crap) but they were doing the job here, paving the way for a [Mahamoti Djinn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mahamoti+Djinn) to leave Ruel in really big trouble.

A [Flowstone Slide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503171056/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flowstone+Slide) dealt with the minor nuisances, but couldn't approach the high toughness of the Djinn. There wasn't much in Ruel's deck that could and two turns later Linov had taken both the game and match.

Matvey Linov beats Antoine Ruel 3-2 and is the winner of the Moscow Invitational.
